title:	[health-check][bug][fp-d93575c] skf-quick-skill: hardcoded src/ helper path breaks installed users
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	bug, fp-d93575c, health-check, workflow-improvement
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	257
--
## Workflow
`skf-quick-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md`

## Severity
`bug`
<!-- bug: instructions were wrong or contradictory -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-d93575c`
<!-- Deterministic dedup key: sha1(severity|workflow|step_file|section-slug)[:7]. -->

## Finding
Step-06 §1 hardcodes the atomic-flip helper as `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py`, a path that only exists in development mode and not in installed mode.

## Expected
The instruction should resolve the helper path the same way `shared/health-check.md` does — relative to the SKF module root (`_bmad/skf/` when installed, `src/` during development).

## Actual
The literal `src/shared/scripts/...` does not exist in installed projects; the actual helper sits at `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` (or `.claude/skills/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` for IDE deployments).

## Evidence
- `src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md:28` — `"python3 {project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py flip-link \"`
- `src/shared/health-check.md:13` — `"All shared/ paths resolve relative to the SKF module root (_bmad/skf/ when installed, src/ during development)"` (the convention quick-skill step-06 fails to follow)
- In an installed repo: `[ -f "src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py" ]` returns false; the helper is found only via glob under `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/` and `.claude/skills/shared/scripts/`.

## Impact
Required an out-of-band glob to locate the helper before the active-pointer flip could run; a less-careful executor would have hit a literal-path failure and either skipped the active pointer or fallen back to the `rm` + `ln -s` pattern that the same step explicitly forbids.

## Suggested Fix
In `src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` §1, replace the hardcoded `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` with the same `shared/...` resolution convention used by `shared/health-check.md`, e.g. *"call the atomic-flip helper at `{skf_module_root}/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` — `{skf_module_root}` is `_bmad/skf/` in installed mode and `src/` during development."* Alternatively, hoist a shared `atomicWriteHelper` path variable into step frontmatter the way `nextStepFile` is hoisted, so the resolution is uniform across all SKF workflows.

## Environment
| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Date | 2026-04-30 |
| OS | Windows 11 Enterprise 10.0.22631 |
| AI Editor | Claude Code |
| Model | Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) |
| Forge Tier | Quick |
| SKF Version | 1.2.0 |

6	
7	## STEP GOAL:
8	
9	To finalize the skill by creating the active-version pointer, displaying the completion summary, and writing the result contract. Deliverables (SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json) were already written in step-05 so that validation could run against files on disk; this step only performs the post-write finalization.
10	
11	## Rules
12	
13	- Do not rewrite deliverables — they were written and validated in step-05
14	- Create the active pointer via the shared helper — never `rm` + `ln -s` manually
15	- Result contract writing is mandatory (pipeline consumers depend on it)
16	
17	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
18	
19	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
20	
21	### 1. Create Active Pointer (atomic flip, Windows-safe)
22	
23	`{skill_group}` and `{skill_package}` were computed in step-05 §1 from `{skills_output_folder}`, `{repo_name}`, and `{version}`; `{version}` was resolved from the extraction inventory. Reuse the same values here — do not recompute.
24	
25	Create or update the `active` pointer at `{skill_group}/active` pointing to `{version}` using the shared atomic-flip helper. The helper acquires an `flock` on `{skill_group}/active.skf-lock`, refuses to replace a non-link at `{skill_group}/active` (protecting against accidental `rm -rf` of a real directory), and uses a rename-over-symlink pattern so the update is atomic from a concurrent reader's perspective. On Windows the helper automatically falls back to a directory junction (`mklink /J`) when `os.symlink` fails with `PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD` / `ACCESS_DENIED` — junctions require no admin elevation and resolve identically for `skf-skill-inventory`'s consumers:
26	
27	```bash
28	python3 {project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py flip-link \
29	  --link {skill_group}/active \
30	  --target {version}
31	```
32	
33	The helper returns non-zero (exit 2) if `{skill_group}/active` already exists as a real directory or file rather than a link — in that case, halt with: "Refusing to flip `{skill_group}/active` — existing path is not a symlink or junction. Investigate manually; expected a link pointing at a version directory." A common cause on Windows is a prior run that executed `ln -s` under git-bash without Developer Mode enabled, which silently wrote a full directory copy; remove that copy and retry.
34	
35	**Never `rm` + `ln -s` the active pointer manually.** The bare-rm pattern has two failure modes: (1) a concurrent reader sees a missing `active` mid-flip, and (2) a bug or typo that replaces `{skill_group}/active` with a plain directory turns the next manual `rm -rf {skill_group}/active` into data loss. The helper encapsulates both guards and the Windows junction fallback.
36	
37	Confirm: "Active pointer: {skill_group}/active -> {version} ({kind})" where `{kind}` is `symlink` or `junction` as returned by the helper.
38	
39	### 2. Display Completion Summary
40	
41	"**Quick Skill complete.**
42	
43	**Skill:** {repo_name} v{version}
44	**Language:** {language}
45	**Source:** {resolved_url}
46	**Authority:** community
47	**Confidence:** {extraction confidence}
48	
49	**Files written:**
50	- `{skill_package}/SKILL.md`
51	- `{skill_package}/context-snippet.md`
52	- `{skill_package}/metadata.json`
53	- `{skill_group}/active` -> `{version}`
54	
55	**Exports documented:** {count}
56	**Validation:** {pass / N issues (advisory)}
57	
58	---
59	
60	**Recommended next steps:**
61	
62	1. **test-skill** (advisory) — Run cognitive completeness verification on the generated skill
63	2. **export-skill** — Package and distribute the skill with platform-aware context injection
64	
65	**Note:** This is a best-effort community skill. For deeper analysis with AST-verified exports and provenance tracking, use the full **create-skill** workflow with a skill brief."
66	
67	### 3. Result Contract
68	
69	Write the result contract per `shared/references/output-contract-schema.md`: the per-run record at `{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-{YYYYMMDD-HHmmss}.json` (UTC timestamp, resolution to seconds) and a copy at `{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-latest.json` (stable path for pipeline consumers — copy, not symlink). Include `SKILL.md`, `context-snippet.md`, and `metadata.json` paths in `outputs` and export count in `summary`.
70	
71	### 4. Chain to Health Check
72

74

12	
13	# Step 7: Generate Artifacts
14	
15	## STEP GOAL:
16	
17	To write all compiled content to disk — 4 deliverable files to `{skill_package}` and 3 workspace artifacts to `{forge_version}`, creating directories as needed. Then create or update the `active` symlink.
18	
19	## Rules
20	
21	- Focus only on writing files from compiled content — do not modify content during writing
22	- All base artifact types must be written (4 deliverables + 3 workspace files + N reference files)
23	- Create directories before writing files
24	
25	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
26	
27	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
28	
29	### 1. Create Directory Structure
30	
31	Resolve `{version}` from the skill brief's `version` field. Create the following directories:
32	
33	```
34	{skill_group}                          # {skills_output_folder}/{name}/
35	{skill_package}                        # {skills_output_folder}/{name}/{version}/{name}/
36	{skill_package}/references/
37	{forge_version}                        # {forge_data_folder}/{name}/{version}/
38	```
39	
40	If `scripts_inventory` is non-empty, also create: `{skill_package}/scripts/`
41	If `assets_inventory` is non-empty, also create: `{skill_package}/assets/`
42	
43	Where `{name}` is the skill name from the brief (kebab-case) and `{version}` is the semver version from the brief (with build metadata stripped per `knowledge/version-paths.md`).
44	
45	If directories already exist, do not error — proceed with file writing (overwrites existing files).
46	
47	### 2. Write Deliverables to {skill_package}
48	
49	Write these 4 files from the compiled content:
50	
51	**File 1:** `{skill_package}/SKILL.md`
52	- The complete compiled skill document
53	- agentskills.io-compliant format with all sections
54	- [MANUAL] markers seeded
55	
56	**File 2:** `{skill_package}/context-snippet.md`
57	- Compressed 2-line format for CLAUDE.md integration
58	
59	**File 3:** `{skill_package}/metadata.json`
60	- Machine-readable birth certificate with stats and provenance
61	
62	**File 4:** `{skill_package}/references/*.md`
63	- One file per function group or type
64	- Progressive disclosure detail files
65	
66	**Files 4b (conditional):** `{skill_package}/scripts/*`
67	- One file per detected script, copied from source with content preserved
68	- Only created when `scripts_inventory` is non-empty
69	
70	**Files 4c (conditional):** `{skill_package}/assets/*`
71	- One file per detected asset, copied from source with content preserved
72	- Only created when `assets_inventory` is non-empty
73	
74	**Note on `file_type: "doc"` entries** (promoted authoritative docs from step-03 §2a):
75	
76	Promoted docs are tracked in `file_entries[]` with `file_type: "doc"` for drift detection but are **NOT** copied into the skill package. The source file remains at its original location outside `{skill_package}`. Step-07 must skip any `file_entries[]` row where `file_type == "doc"` when iterating for file copy — these entries exist only for provenance tracking, not bundling. Step-07 verification (§5) also does not check for doc files in the skill package.
77	
78	### 3. Write Workspace Artifacts to {forge_version}
79	
80	Write these 3 files from the compiled content:
81	
82	**File 5:** `{forge_version}/provenance-map.json`
83	- Per-claim source map with AST bindings and confidence tiers
84	
85	**File 6:** `{forge_version}/evidence-report.md`
86	- Build artifact with extraction summary, validation results, warnings
87	
88	**File 7:** `{forge_version}/extraction-rules.yaml`
89	- Language and ast-grep schema used for this extraction (for reproducibility)
90	- Note: This file is generated here from extraction data collected during steps 3-4, not assembled in step-05
91	
92	### 4. Create Active Symlink (atomic flip)
93	
94	Create or update the `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active` pointing to `{version}` using the shared atomic-flip helper. The helper holds an `flock` on `{skill_group}/active.skf-lock`, refuses to replace a non-symlink at `{skill_group}/active` (protecting against accidental rm-rf of a real directory), and uses a rename-over-symlink pattern so the update is atomic from a concurrent reader's perspective:
95	
96	```bash
97	python3 {atomicWriteHelper} flip-link \
98	  --link {skill_group}/active \
99	  --target {version}
100	```
101	
102	The helper returns non-zero (exit 2) if `{skill_group}/active` already exists as a real directory or file rather than a symlink — in that case, halt with: "Refusing to flip `{skill_group}/active` — existing path is not a symlink. Investigate manually; expected a symlink pointing at a version directory."
103	
104	**Never `rm` + `ln -s` the active link manually.** The bare-rm pattern has two failure modes: (1) a concurrent reader sees a missing `active` mid-flip, and (2) a bug or typo that replaces `{skill_group}/active` with a plain directory turns the next manual `rm -rf {skill_group}/active` into data loss. The helper encapsulates both guards.
105	
106	### 5. Verify Write Completion
107	
108	After all files are written, verify:
109	- All 4 deliverable artifact types exist (SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json, at least one file in references/), all 3 workspace artifacts exist (provenance-map.json, evidence-report.md, extraction-rules.yaml), plus scripts/ and assets/ files when inventories are non-empty
110	- The `active` symlink at `{skill_group}/active` resolves to `{version}`
```

12	# Step 6: Finalize
13	
14	## STEP GOAL:
15	
16	To finalize the skill by creating the active-version pointer, displaying the completion summary, and writing the result contract. Deliverables (SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json) were already written in step-05 so that validation could run against files on disk; this step only performs the post-write finalization.
17	
18	## Rules
19	
20	- Do not rewrite deliverables — they were written and validated in step-05
21	- Create the active pointer via the shared helper — never `rm` + `ln -s` manually
22	- Result contract writing is mandatory (pipeline consumers depend on it)
23	
24	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
25	
26	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
27	
28	### 1. Create Active Pointer (atomic flip, Windows-safe)
29	
30	`{skill_group}` and `{skill_package}` were computed in step-05 §1 from `{skills_output_folder}`, `{repo_name}`, and `{version}`; `{version}` was resolved from the extraction inventory. Reuse the same values here — do not recompute.
31	
32	Create or update the `active` pointer at `{skill_group}/active` pointing to `{version}` using the shared atomic-flip helper. The helper acquires an `flock` on `{skill_group}/active.skf-lock`, refuses to replace a non-link at `{skill_group}/active` (protecting against accidental `rm -rf` of a real directory), and uses a rename-over-symlink pattern so the update is atomic from a concurrent reader's perspective. On Windows the helper automatically falls back to a directory junction (`mklink /J`) when `os.symlink` fails with `PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD` / `ACCESS_DENIED` — junctions require no admin elevation and resolve identically for `skf-skill-inventory`'s consumers:
33	
34	**Resolve `{atomicWriteHelper}`:** probe `{atomicWriteProbeOrder}` (installed `_bmad/skf/...` first, dev `src/...` fallback); first existing path wins. HALT if neither candidate exists — the active-pointer flip MUST go through the atomic helper.
35	
36	```bash
37	python3 {atomicWriteHelper} flip-link \
38	  --link {skill_group}/active \
39	  --target {version}
40	```
41	
42	The helper returns non-zero (exit 2) if `{skill_group}/active` already exists as a real directory or file rather than a link — in that case, halt with: "Refusing to flip `{skill_group}/active` — existing path is not a symlink or junction. Investigate manually; expected a link pointing at a version directory." A common cause on Windows is a prior run that executed `ln -s` under git-bash without Developer Mode enabled, which silently wrote a full directory copy; remove that copy and retry.
43	
44	**Never `rm` + `ln -s` the active pointer manually.** The bare-rm pattern has two failure modes: (1) a concurrent reader sees a missing `active` mid-flip, and (2) a bug or typo that replaces `{skill_group}/active` with a plain directory turns the next manual `rm -rf {skill_group}/active` into data loss. The helper encapsulates both guards and the Windows junction fallback.
45

1	---
2	name: quality-analysis
3	description: Comprehensive quality analysis for BMad workflows and skills. Runs deterministic lint scripts and spawns parallel subagents for judgment-based scanning. Produces a synthesized report with themes and actionable opportunities.
4	menu-code: QA
5	---
6	
7	# Quality Analysis
8	
9	Communicate with user in `{communication_language}`. Write report content in `{document_output_language}`.
10	
11	You orchestrate quality analysis on a BMad workflow or skill. Deterministic checks run as scripts (fast, zero tokens). Judgment-based analysis runs as LLM subagents. A report creator synthesizes everything into a unified, theme-based report.
12	
13	## Your Role: Coordination, Not File Reading
14	
15	**DO NOT read the target skill's files yourself.** Scripts and subagents do all analysis.
16	
17	You orchestrate: run deterministic scripts and pre-pass extractors, spawn LLM scanner subagents in parallel, then hand off to the report creator for synthesis.
18	
19	## Headless Mode
20	
21	If `{headless_mode}=true`, skip all user interaction, use safe defaults, note any warnings, and output structured JSON as specified in the Present Findings section.
22	
23	## Pre-Scan Checks
24	
25	Check for uncommitted changes. In headless mode, note warnings and proceed. In interactive mode, inform the user and confirm before proceeding. In interactive mode, also confirm the workflow is currently functioning.
26	
27	## Analysis Principles
28	
29	**Effectiveness over efficiency.** The analysis may suggest leaner phrasing, but if the current phrasing captures the right guidance, it should be kept. Over-optimization can make skills lose their effectiveness. The report presents opportunities — the user applies judgment.
30	
31	## Scanners
32	
33	### Lint Scripts (Deterministic — Run First)
34	
35	These run instantly, cost zero tokens, and produce structured JSON:
36	
37	| #   | Script                           | Focus                                   | Output File                |
38	| --- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
39	| S1  | `scripts/scan-path-standards.py` | Path conventions                        | `path-standards-temp.json` |
40	| S2  | `scripts/scan-scripts.py`        | Script portability, PEP 723, unit tests | `scripts-temp.json`        |
41	
42	### Pre-Pass Scripts (Feed LLM Scanners)
43	
44	Extract metrics so LLM scanners work from compact data instead of raw files:
45	
46	| #   | Script                                  | Feeds                        | Output File                       |
47	| --- | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
48	| P1  | `scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py` | workflow-integrity scanner   | `workflow-integrity-prepass.json` |
49	| P2  | `scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py`     | prompt-craft scanner         | `prompt-metrics-prepass.json`     |
50	| P3  | `scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py`     | execution-efficiency scanner | `execution-deps-prepass.json`     |
51	
52	### LLM Scanners (Judgment-Based — Run After Scripts)
53	
54	Each scanner writes a free-form analysis document (not JSON):
55	
56	| #   | Scanner                                     | Focus                                                                     | Pre-Pass? | Output File                             |
57	| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------- |
58	| L1  | `quality-scan-workflow-integrity.md`        | Structural completeness, naming, type-appropriate requirements            | Yes       | `workflow-integrity-analysis.md`        |
59	| L2  | `quality-scan-prompt-craft.md`              | Token efficiency, outcome-driven balance, progressive disclosure, pruning | Yes       | `prompt-craft-analysis.md`              |
60	| L3  | `quality-scan-execution-efficiency.md`      | Parallelization, subagent delegation, context optimization                | Yes       | `execution-efficiency-analysis.md`      |
61	| L4  | `quality-scan-skill-cohesion.md`            | Stage flow, purpose alignment, complexity appropriateness                 | No        | `skill-cohesion-analysis.md`            |
62	| L5  | `quality-scan-enhancement-opportunities.md` | Edge cases, UX gaps, user journeys, headless potential                    | No        | `enhancement-opportunities-analysis.md` |
63	| L6  | `quality-scan-script-opportunities.md`      | Deterministic operations that should be scripts                           | No        | `script-opportunities-analysis.md`      |
64	
65	## Execution
66	
67	First create output directory: `{bmad_builder_reports}/{skill-name}/quality-analysis/{date-time-stamp}/`
68	
69	### Step 1: Run All Scripts (Parallel)
70	
71	Run all lint scripts and pre-pass scripts in parallel:
72	
73	```bash
74	python3 scripts/scan-path-standards.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/path-standards-temp.json
75	python3 scripts/scan-scripts.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/scripts-temp.json
76	uv run scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/workflow-integrity-prepass.json
77	python3 scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/prompt-metrics-prepass.json
78	uv run scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/execution-deps-prepass.json
79	```
80	
81	### Step 2: Spawn LLM Scanners (Parallel)
82	
83	After scripts complete, spawn all applicable LLM scanners as parallel subagents.
84	
85	**For scanners WITH pre-pass (L1, L2, L3):** provide the pre-pass JSON file path so the scanner reads compact metrics first, then reads raw files only as needed for judgment calls.
86	
87	**For scanners WITHOUT pre-pass (L4, L5, L6):** provide just the skill path and output directory.
88	
89	Each subagent receives:
90	
91	- Scanner file to load
92	- Skill path: `{skill-path}`
93	- Output directory: `{report-dir}`
94	- Pre-pass file path (if applicable)
95	
96	The subagent loads the scanner file, analyzes the skill, writes its analysis to the output directory, and returns the filename.
97	
98	### Step 3: Synthesize Report
99	
100	After all scanners complete, spawn a subagent with `./report-quality-scan-creator.md`.
101	
102	Provide:
103	
104	- `{skill-path}` — The skill being analyzed
105	- `{quality-report-dir}` — Directory containing all scanner output
106	
107	The report creator reads everything, synthesizes themes, and writes:
108	
109	1. `quality-report.md` — Narrative markdown report
110	2. `report-data.json` — Structured data for HTML
111	
112	### Step 4: Generate HTML Report
113	
114	After the report creator finishes, generate the interactive HTML:
115	
116	```bash
117	python3 scripts/generate-html-report.py {report-dir} --open
118	```
119	
120	This reads `report-data.json` and produces `quality-report.html` — a self-contained interactive report with opportunity themes, "Fix This Theme" prompt generation, and expandable detailed analysis.
121	
122	## Present to User
123	
124	**IF `{headless_mode}=true`:**
125	
126	Read `report-data.json` and output:
127	
128	```json
129	{
130	  "headless_mode": true,
131	  "scan_completed": true,
132	  "report_file": "{path}/quality-report.md",
133	  "html_report": "{path}/quality-report.html",
134	  "data_file": "{path}/report-data.json",
135	  "warnings": [],
136	  "grade": "Excellent|Good|Fair|Poor",
137	  "opportunities": 0,
138	  "broken": 0
139	}
140	```
141	
142	**IF interactive:**
143	
144	Read `report-data.json` and present:
145	
146	1. Grade and narrative — the 2-3 sentence synthesis
147	2. Broken items (if any) — critical/high issues prominently
148	3. Top opportunities — theme names with finding counts and impact
149	4. Reports — "Full report: quality-report.md" and "Interactive HTML opened in browser"
150	5. Offer: apply fixes directly, use HTML to select specific items, or discuss findings
151

GRADE: Good
NARRATIVE: skf-quick-skill is a coherent, well-bounded seven-step workflow with strong purpose alignment, disciplined progressive disclosure, and best-in-class headless gate semantics. Its principal opportunity is intelligence placement: the LLM is currently asked to hand-walk multi-registry resolution, parse six manifest formats, regex-extract exports across six languages, and assemble strict-shape JSON envelopes — work that scripts (some already shipped in src/shared/scripts/) would do deterministically and for ~2,700-3,800 fewer tokens per invocation.

--- BROKEN ---
  [high] Bare _bmad reference without {project-root} prefix
  [high] Bare _bmad reference without {project-root} prefix

--- OPPORTUNITIES ---
  • None (impact: Saves ~2,730-3,870 tokens per invocation prompt-side plus substantial raw-payload reduction; eliminates a class of hand-written-JSON bugs; reinforces the skill's `fast` positioning and frees the LLM for genuine judgment work (trigger-optimized descriptions, usage-pattern synthesis, gotchas)., findings: 10)
  • None (impact: Improves isolation-resilience under context compaction, restores prepass signal accuracy, and tightens the per-step Rules signal., findings: 13)

7	
8	**Good** — `skf-quick-skill` is a coherent, well-bounded seven-step workflow with strong purpose alignment, disciplined progressive disclosure, and best-in-class headless gate semantics. Its principal opportunity is intelligence placement: the LLM is currently asked to hand-walk multi-registry resolution, parse six manifest formats, regex-extract exports across six languages, and assemble strict-shape JSON envelopes — work that scripts (some already shipped in `src/shared/scripts/`) would do deterministically and for ~2,700-3,800 fewer tokens per invocation.

17	### 1. Intelligence placement: deterministic work asked of the LLM (high — 10 observations)
18	
19	The skill correctly delegates headline validation to `npx skill-check` and active-pointer flipping to `skf-atomic-write.py`, but it still asks the LLM to parse three registry JSON payloads, parse six manifest formats, regex-extract exports across six languages, hand-assemble a 30-field strict-shape `metadata.json` (with documented integer-vs-string landmines), and hand-write a timestamped result-contract envelope. Two of those last items have shipped helpers in `src/shared/scripts/` that the skill simply doesn't call. Pushing this work into scripts reinforces the "fast" positioning rather than compromising it: scripts are faster than token-by-token assembly, and they free the LLM to do the genuinely judgment-driven parts (trigger-optimized descriptions, usage-pattern synthesis, notable gotchas).
20	
21	**Fix:** (1) Wire in the existing helpers immediately — replace step-06 §3 with a call to `skf-emit-result-envelope.py`, and replace the step-05 manual-fallback frontmatter checklist with `skf-validate-frontmatter.py`. (2) Build three new shared scripts — `skf-resolve-registry.py`, `skf-extract-manifest.py`, `skf-scan-exports.py` — and have the LLM consume their compact JSON output. (3) Replace the inline metadata.json template in step-04 with `skf-compile-metadata.py` driven from the extraction inventory.

36	### 2. Stage prompt structural conventions (medium — 10 observations)
37	
38	Every stage prompt is missing the standard config/language header (so a stage loaded in isolation has no `{communication_language}` / `{document_output_language}` in scope), three stages do not use scanner-recognised progression vocabulary (the substance is correct — "ONLY WHEN … will you load …" — but the prepass cannot detect it), and a handful of polite/vague phrasings ("please check", "when ready") remain in user-facing copy. None of these are runtime defects, but they reduce isolation-resilience and dilute the signal of the per-step `## Rules` blocks.
39	
40	**Fix:** Add a uniform 2-3 line config header to each step (Language + Output Language for the doc-writing steps), reformat the three "ONLY WHEN" blocks as labelled `Progression` subsections using the standard pattern, replace polite "please" with direct imperatives in HARD HALT user copy, and rephrase "When ready, continue to validation" as "Review the output above, then select [C] to continue."

58	### 3. Headless and CI orchestration primitives (medium — 5 observations)
59	
60	Headless mode is excellent at the gate level (every gate carries an explicit `[default: X]` and logs the auto-decision), but it lacks orchestration-level primitives that turn the skill from "headless-capable" into a first-class CI building block: there is no machine-readable error envelope for hard-halt paths (no result contract is written when step-06 never runs), no structured exit code contract distinguishing resolution / write / validation / health-check failures, no `--on-collision` policy (today's silent force-overwrite is a footgun for batched runs), no `--on-ecosystem-match` policy, and no stdout advertisement of the result-contract path.
61	
62	**Fix:** Add four headless primitives — (a) emit a single deterministic stdout line `QUICK_SKILL_RESULT=<path>` from step-06, (b) define structured exit codes (0 success, 2 validation issues, 3 resolution failure, 4 write failure, 5 health-check failure) and emit a minimal error envelope on every hard-halt path so automators always have a JSON to parse, (c) accept `--on-collision={overwrite,skip,fail}` (default `fail` headless to avoid silent clobber of manual edits), and (d) accept `--on-ecosystem-match={proceed,install,abort,fail}`.
63

67	- No structured exit code contract — enhancement-opportunities (Automator journey)
68	- step-05 §1 overwrite gate force-overwrites silently in headless — enhancement-opportunities high-opportunity (collision policy)
69	- Result-contract path not advertised on stdout — enhancement-opportunities medium-opportunity
70	- step-02 ecosystem-match auto-[P] regardless of automator preference — enhancement-opportunities (Automator journey)
71	
72	### 4. Wall-clock latency on independent network reads (low — 4 observations)
73	
74	Several pre-pass operations execute as sequential blocking calls when they could be batched: step-03 reads README, manifest, and entry-point file as three sequential round trips against the same `?ref={source_ref}`; step-01's registry-fallback chain (npm → PyPI → crates.io → web search) is sequential blocking; step-05 invokes `npx skill-check` twice paying the npx startup cost each time; activation reads `config.yaml` and `preferences.yaml` sequentially. Token impact is negligible — this is a wall-clock-time win, especially material on the multi-module Java/Kotlin scan loops.
75	
76	**Fix:** Add a one-line directive at the top of step-03's MANDATORY SEQUENCE noting that §1, §2, and §3 read independent files and should be batched in a single tool-call message, with an explicit "fetch all module manifests in parallel" note for the multi-module branch. In step-05, run a single `npx skill-check check ... --fix --format json` invocation and skip §8 (security scan is enabled by default). Add per-call timeouts (10s registry, 15s GitHub content) to bound hostile-environment behavior.

81	- step-05 §4 and §8 invoke `npx skill-check` twice — execution-efficiency F2
82	- SKILL.md activation reads config.yaml and preferences.yaml sequentially — execution-efficiency F4
83	- step-01 registry fallback chain is sequential blocking with no per-call timeout — enhancement-opportunities (Hostile Environment)
84	
85	### 5. Edge-case coverage and graceful redirection (medium — 7 observations)
86	
87	The skill assumes every target is a clean library on a public GitHub repo, but the real world is monorepos, mega-repos, applications-not-libraries, free-form prose typed by confused users, and offline/proxy environments. Several real journeys land in unhelpful places: a confused user typing "I want to brainstorm a skill" gets a registry-failure HARD HALT with no sibling-skill suggestion; `lodash` produces a 300-export wall (no cap on Key Exports); npm/pnpm/cargo workspaces and Python namespace packages get root-resolution despite Java/Kotlin already handling multi-module; the atomic-helper miss in step-06 hard-halts with files already on disk (no completion summary, no result contract); and validation-issue reporting auto-proceeds with no "want me to try to fix?" loop.
88	
89	**Fix:** Add (a) a confused-user redirect at step-01 §3 that classifies the input and suggests `skf-create-skill` or `bmad-agent-builder` when no package-like or URL-like token is present, (b) a soft pre-flight skill-candidacy sniff between resolve and extract (library/monorepo-root/application/cli/unknown) with a scope-hint prompt for monorepos, (c) a soft cap of 30-50 entries on Key Exports with namespace grouping above the cap, (d) workspace detection for npm/pnpm/cargo/Python via `package.json` workspaces, `Cargo.toml` `[workspace]`, `pyproject.toml` `[tool.uv.workspace]`, (e) graceful degradation in step-06 (fall back to `os.symlink` with loud warning when atomic helper is missing), and (f) a soft "[F] try to fix and revalidate / [C] continue / [A] abort" gate when validation reports advisory issues.
90

94	- No pre-flight skill-candidacy sniff before extraction — enhancement-opportunities high-opportunity
95	- No upper bound on Key Exports section — enhancement-opportunities medium-opportunity
96	- Workspace/monorepo detection only implemented for Java/Kotlin — enhancement-opportunities medium-opportunity

98	- Validation issues reported but no fix-loop — enhancement-opportunities medium-opportunity
99	- No "successfully-resolved-but-empty-repo" path — skill-cohesion (Gap & Redundancy, medium)
100	
101	## Strengths
102	
103	- **Tight purpose alignment.** Every word of the description ("Fast", "no brief needed", "best-effort", "community", "tier-unaware") is honored by the actual flow. The promises-vs-behavior trace holds.
104	- **Disciplined progressive disclosure.** SKILL.md stays at 57 lines / ~705 tokens because every detail lives in the right place: routing table for stages, frontmatter chain for stage progression, dedicated reference for registry resolution, dedicated asset for output schemas. Textbook structure for a multi-stage skill.
105	- **Self-contained stage prompts.** Each `steps-c/step-*.md` opens with `## STEP GOAL`, `## Rules`, `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE` and closes with `## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE`. None rely on SKILL.md being in context to execute correctly — strong context-compaction survival.
106	- **Explicit headless gates.** Every interactive gate carries `**GATE [default: X]**` plus an audit log line. The agent doesn't infer headless behavior — it's documented at each gate site. This is best-in-class.
107	- **Excellent dependency-failure hygiene.** `atomicWriteHelper` resolution in step-06, empirical `npx skill-check -h` probe in step-05, registry-fallback chain in step-01 — all share the same probe → fall back → hard-halt-with-actionable-message pattern.
108	- **Pragmatic write-before-validate ordering with rationale spelled out.** Step-05 writes artifacts before validating because `skill-check` is a file-based CLI; the comment in step-05 §1 documents the choice for future maintainers. A model for non-obvious decisions.
109	- **Advisory-vs-blocking distinction is consistent.** Ecosystem check, validation, security scan, test-skill all advisory; resolution failure, write failure, missing atomic helper all hard halt. The skill is opinionated about which gates can fail soft.
110	- **Multi-language extraction coverage with thoughtful Java/Kotlin multi-module handling.** JS/TS, Python, Rust, Go, Java (Maven multi-module), Kotlin (Gradle multi-project) all covered.
111	- **Low-confidence escape hatch.** "This skill was generated with limited source data. Consider running create-skill..." acknowledges limits without overclaiming.
112	- **Clean stage manifest.** All 7 referenced stages exist on disk under `steps-c/`; no orphans, no missing, sequential numbering with descriptive names.
113	- **Frontmatter is conformant.** `name: skf-quick-skill` matches the folder, `skf-` module prefix is correct, description follows two-part format with quoted specific triggers.
114	
115	## Detailed Analysis
116	
117	### Structure & Integrity
118	
119	Structurally the skill is sound: complete and consistent stage manifest (7 referenced, 7 actual, zero missing/orphaned), required SKILL.md sections in logical order (Overview → Role → Workflow Rules → Stages → Invocation Contract → On Activation), workflow type claim ("complex") matches actual structure, and activation sequence is well-ordered (config load → headless resolve → load step-01). Two `_bmad/` references are missing the `{project-root}/` prefix (covered in What's Broken). Stage-prompt config-header and progression-keyword findings are covered in Theme 2.
120	
121	### Craft & Writing Quality
122	
123	**Overview quality:** Appropriate. SKILL.md Overview is a 3-line paragraph that establishes mission, input contract, deliverables, no-brief positioning, and the tier-unaware design rationale; Role + Workflow Rules carry additional framing. **Progressive disclosure:** Excellent. 57 lines / ~705 tokens, no inlined data tables, no fenced reference blocks, clean delegation to `steps-c/*` and `references/`/`assets/`. Remaining findings:
124	
125	- **SKILL.md:44 Invocation Contract gate row breaks markdown rendering** — uses literal `|` characters as separators inside a 2-column table cell, which the markdown renderer parses as 4 extra cells. The Invocation Contract is the row most likely to be scanned by integrators. Fix: replace inner `|` with `;` or `<br>`, or break the row into a sub-bullet list.
126	- **Repeated boilerplate "CRITICAL: Follow this sequence exactly" across 6 of 7 step files** (~30 tokens × 6 ≈ 180 tokens). The per-step `## Rules` and `MANDATORY SEQUENCE` heading already convey ordering intent. Fix: delete the repeated line or move to SKILL.md Workflow Rules once.
127	- **Repeated boilerplate "ONLY WHEN ... will you load and read fully {nextStepFile}"** restated 2-3 times per step (Menu Handling Logic, EXECUTION RULES, CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE). Keep the canonical NOTE; trim earlier blocks to single-line directives.
128	- **step-06 frontmatter contains a 6-line block comment** explaining `{atomicWriteHelper}` resolution; the body restates the same logic at line 34. Drop the frontmatter comment.
129	- **SKILL.md:20-23 defensive imperatives in Workflow Rules** ("do not skip, reorder, or optimize", "never preload future steps") read as workflow-engine cargo-cult. Optional fix: rewrite positively.
130	- **Pre-pass false negative.** prepass-prompt-metrics reported `total_prompts: 0` because it only inspects root-level `*.md`; the seven `steps-c/` files are present and well-formed. Tooling gap, not a skill defect.
131	
132	### Cohesion & Design
133	
134	Coherent, tightly-scoped workflow that delivers exactly what its name promises. Seven-step pipeline reads as a single narrative — resolve → check → extract → compile → write+validate → finalize → health-check — with each step producing inputs the next consumes.
135	
136	- **Stage Flow Coherence:** Strong. No dead-end stages, no produce/consume mismatches, no ambiguous entry points. Single review gate at step-04 → 5 is well-placed (review compiled output before bytes land on disk).
137	- **Purpose Alignment:** Strong. Description, Overview rationale, and Workflow Rules all enforced by step instructions.
138	- **Complexity Appropriateness:** Strong. Seven steps is on the higher end for a "quick" path but every step earns its place; step-07 is delegation-only (~25 lines).
139	- **Gap & Redundancy:** Moderate. Covered in Theme 5 (edge-case coverage) plus: `scope_hint` is accepted but its honoring is invisible in outputs (medium); manifest-detection table duplicated between step-01 §4 and `references/registry-resolution.md` (low drift risk).
140	- **Dependency Graph Logic:** Moderate. **Outputs declaration omits the result contract** (medium): SKILL.md Outputs row reads "SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json, active symlink" but step-06 §3 mandates writing `quick-skill-result-{ts}.json` + `quick-skill-result-latest.json`. Pipeline consumers depend on the latest pointer; missing this from the contract table means a downstream skill author reading SKILL.md alone might not know to consume it. Fix: add "result contract (timestamped + latest)" to Outputs.
141	- **External Skill Integration:** Strong. agentskills.io advisory check with timeout, empirical `npx skill-check -h` probe with manual fallback, clean delegation to `shared/health-check.md` with the relative-path gotcha documented.
142	
143	**Step naming friction (low):** Step-05 is "Write & Validate" but the file is `step-05-validate.md`; step-06 is "Finalize" but the file is `step-06-write.md`. Renaming the files to match step titles (or vice versa) would remove one moment of grep confusion.
144	
145	### Execution Efficiency
146	
147	Pre-pass found zero issues across all severities; deeper read confirms that finding is correct rather than a false negative. The skill processes one repository at a time, reads ~5 files, produces three small artifacts — subagent delegation thresholds are not crossed; do not introduce them. All findings are low-severity wall-clock-time wins covered in Theme 4. Already-efficient patterns to preserve: minimal activation (no resource pre-loading), lazy reference loading via frontmatter pointers, single-source-of-truth for `{skill_group}` / `{skill_package}` / `{version}` (step-06 §1 explicitly says "Reuse the same values — do not recompute"), `atomicWriteHelper` probe order resolved once in frontmatter, step-02's 5s timeout on advisory ecosystem queries, fail-fast ordering (resolution halt before extraction; review gate before disk-write commit), and per-step explicit headless gate defaults.
148	
149	### User Experience
150	
151	Headless-ready at the gate level (best-in-class), but with orchestration-level gaps covered in Theme 3. Six user journeys evaluated:
152	
153	- **First-Timer:** Pastes a giant repo URL (e.g. `microsoft/vscode`); pipeline cheerfully extracts a mega-app and produces noisy artifacts. Friction: no early candidacy sniff, scope_hint discoverability low. Bright spot: README fallback in step-03 §4 means even hopeless targets produce something.
154	- **Expert:** Headless flies through gates; arg-pass-through and version syntax are clean. Friction: no batch mode, no "always approve" session flag, redundant ecosystem checks across runs. Bright spot: headless contract is sharper than most BMad workflows.
155	- **Confused User:** Types prose ("I want a skill that helps with onboarding"), gets registry-failure HARD HALT with no sibling-skill suggestion. Highest-impact single fix in the whole report.
156	- **Edge-Case (lodash):** 300-export wall, no grouping, no namespace categorization. Java/Kotlin multi-module path is the model — needs to propagate to npm/pnpm/cargo workspaces and Python namespace packages.
157	- **Hostile Environment:** Sequential blocking registry chain with no per-call timeout; atomic-helper miss leaves orphaned files; no partial-result resumption. Bright spot: step-02's 5s timeout is exactly right.
158	- **Automator:** Headless gate semantics are thoroughly per-step-specified; result contract is documented. Friction: no machine-readable error envelope on hard-halt paths, no structured exit codes, silent force-overwrite collision policy, no aggregate batch-report path.
159	
160	**Autonomous potential:** Headless-ready. Every interactive gate has explicit `[default: X]` headless behavior with logging. Adding the four headless orchestration primitives in Theme 3 would elevate this from "headless-capable" to "first-class CI building block."
161	
162	**Facilitative-pattern observations:** Dual-Output (SKILL.md + context-snippet) is exemplary. Intent-Before-Ingestion is weak — a two-line "what kind of skill — full surface, narrow scope, summary?" would inform extraction priorities and unlock the export-cap suggestion. Graceful Degradation is partial — README fallback and ecosystem timeout are great, atomic-helper hard-halt is the outlier.
163	
164	### Script Opportunities
165	
166	The skill itself ships zero scripts under `src/skf-quick-skill/scripts/` (the directory does not exist). It depends correctly on `skf-atomic-write.py` (resolved via `atomicWriteProbeOrder` in step-06) and the external `npx skill-check` CLI for headline validation, but it does not call two existing shared helpers — `skf-validate-frontmatter.py` and `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` — and there are three new scripts that would push deterministic work out of the LLM and reinforce the skill's "fast" positioning.
167	
168	**Estimated total token savings:** ~2,730-3,870 tokens per invocation prompt-side, plus a substantial raw-payload reduction on F2/F3 (manifests and source files no longer dumped into LLM context).
169	
170	| Script opportunity | Severity | Tokens saved |
171	|---|---|---|
172	| F1 `skf-resolve-registry.py` (new) | High | 600-900 |
173	| F2 `skf-extract-manifest.py` (new) | High | 400-600 + raw payload |
174	| F3 `skf-scan-exports.py` (new) | High | 500-800 + raw source |
175	| F4 `skf-compile-metadata.py` (new) | High | 400-500 |
176	| F5 `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` (already exists) | High | 250-350 |
177	| F6 `skf-validate-frontmatter.py` (already exists) | Medium | 150-250 |
178	| F7 body-section presence (extend `skf-validate-output.py`) | Medium | 150 |
179	| F8 context-snippet token count (new, with `tiktoken`) | Medium | 120 |
180	| F9 version-suffix parsing | Low | 80 (fold into F1) |
181	| F10 language detection by manifest | Low | 80-120 (fold into F1/F2) |
182	
183	The lint-script pre-pass for `scripts/` reported `pass — no scripts/ directory found, nothing to scan`. That is structurally correct (this skill currently relies on shared helpers) and not a defect, but the absence of shipped scripts is what enables the high-severity intelligence-placement findings above.
184	
185	## Recommendations
186	
187	1. **Wire in the two existing shared helpers.** Replace step-06 §3 with a `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` invocation; replace step-05's manual frontmatter checklist with `skf-validate-frontmatter.py`. Resolves 2 high-severity script findings, ~400-600 tokens saved per run, eliminates an entire class of hand-written-JSON bugs. **Effort: low.**
188	2. **Build the three new shared scripts** (`skf-resolve-registry.py`, `skf-extract-manifest.py`, `skf-scan-exports.py`) and rewire steps 01 and 03 to consume their JSON. Resolves 5 high-severity intelligence-placement findings (F1-F3 + F9-F10), plus retires the inline regex/manifest prose. **Effort: medium.**
189	3. **Replace step-04's inline metadata.json template with `skf-compile-metadata.py`** driven by the extraction inventory; eliminates the integer-vs-string class of bugs and removes one of two divergent schema sources. Resolves F4 and prompt-craft M3. **Effort: medium.**
190	4. **Fix the two bare `_bmad/` path references** in step-06 and step-07. **Effort: low.**
191	5. **Add the confused-user redirect at step-01 §3** — one regex classifies input as package/URL-like or not; the prose-typing user gets a sibling-skill suggestion instead of a registry-failure halt. Highest-impact UX single fix. **Effort: low.**
192	6. **Add a uniform 2-3 line config header to all seven step files** (Language + Output Language for doc-writing steps), reformat the three "ONLY WHEN" blocks as labelled `Progression` subsections, and replace polite "please" / vague "when ready" in user-facing copy. Resolves all 13 prepass findings. **Effort: low.**
193	7. **Add the four headless orchestration primitives:** stdout `QUICK_SKILL_RESULT=<path>` from step-06, structured exit codes, error envelope on every hard-halt path, `--on-collision` and `--on-ecosystem-match` policies. Turns the skill into a first-class CI building block. **Effort: medium.**
194	8. **Document parallel-fetch expectations in step-03** (one line at the top of MANDATORY SEQUENCE) and collapse the two `npx skill-check` invocations in step-05 into one. Resolves the wall-clock latency theme. **Effort: low.**
195	9. **Add the SKILL.md Outputs-row entry for the result contract** and the `scope_hint` provenance echo (metadata.json or completion summary). Resolves cohesion-medium findings. **Effort: low.**
196	10. **Fix the SKILL.md:44 Invocation Contract markdown table** (escape inner `|`). Affects the row most likely to be scanned by integrators. **Effort: low.**
197	11. **Edge-case coverage upgrades:** soft pre-flight candidacy sniff, Key Exports cap with namespace grouping, npm/pnpm/cargo/Python workspace detection, atomic-helper graceful degradation, validation-issue fix loop. **Effort: medium-high — phase opportunistically.**
198

Before I dive in: "fix them all" is a non-trivial commitment. Here's what the report actually requires, broken down honestly:

## Scope reality

**Phase 1 — Quick wins (low effort, ~7 commits, ~1-2h)**
- Fix 2 broken `{project-root}/` prefixes (broken)
- Wire in 2 existing shipped helpers (`skf-emit-result-envelope.py`, `skf-validate-frontmatter.py`) — Rec #1
- Add config headers + Progression sections + polite-phrase fixes to all 7 step files — Rec #6 (13 findings)
- Parallel-fetch directive + dedupe `npx skill-check` calls — Rec #8
- SKILL.md Outputs row + Invocation Contract table escape — Rec #9, #10
- Confused-user redirect at step-01 — Rec #5
- `scope_hint` provenance echo

**Phase 2 — New shared scripts (medium effort, ~3-4 commits, ~3-5h)**
- Build `skf-resolve-registry.py`, `skf-extract-manifest.py`, `skf-scan-exports.py`, `skf-compile-metadata.py`
- Each needs unit tests under `.claude/skills/.../scripts/tests/` and to pass `npm run quality`
- Rewire steps 01, 03, 04 to consume their JSON

**Phase 3 — Headless CI primitives (medium effort, ~2-3 commits, ~2-3h)**
- `QUICK_SKILL_RESULT=<path>` stdout line
- Structured exit code contract (0/2/3/4/5)
- Error envelope on every hard-halt path
- `--on-collision={overwrite,skip,fail}` and `--on-ecosystem-match` flags

**Phase 4 — Edge-case UX (medium-high, the report explicitly says "phase opportunistically")**
- Candidacy sniff, Key Exports cap, multi-language workspace detection, atomic-helper graceful degradation, validation fix-loop

**Total realistic estimate:** ~6-10h, 15-20 commits, 4 PRs.

## Risk

The report itself flags Phase 4 as "phase opportunistically" — doing it in one batch risks compromising effectiveness for completeness, which the workflow explicitly warns against. Phase 2 also adds new shared scripts that other SKF workflows could end up depending on, so they need to be built carefully (not rushed).

## Recommended approach

I'd propose: **execute Phases 1-3 now in 3 separate PRs, defer Phase 4 to opportunistic follow-ups via `/schedule`.** That should land "Excellent" on the next scan because Phases 1-3 hit every "high"/"medium" theme. Phase 4 is mostly low-severity and won't block the grade.

**Two questions before I start:**

1. **OK with the phased plan above** (3 PRs now + scheduled follow-ups for Phase 4)? Or do you want one big PR?
2. **Branch strategy:** one branch per phase (cleaner review) or one branch with phase commits (faster)?

If you just say "go" I'll default to: 3 PRs, one per phase, sequenced.

usage: skf-emit-result-envelope.py [-h] {emit,validate} ...

SKF Emit Result Envelope — Schema-locked headless output for skf-setup.

Replaces the prose-driven envelope assembly in `src/skf-setup/steps-c/
step-04-report.md` §4 with one Python invocation. The envelope contract
(SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON) was added to step-04 in PR #247, extended in
PR #248 (header / outputs / failure modes), and extended again in this
PR (previous_tier, tier_changed, tools_added, tools_removed, error).

LLM-rendered envelopes risk silent schema drift on every invocation —
a pipeline that grep's `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON: {…}` out of the workflow
log can break if the model decides to rename a key or rearrange a
nested structure. This script is the single source of truth: it takes
a context payload as JSON on stdin, computes derived fields
deterministically, and emits the envelope in a fixed shape that
matches the JSON Schema at
`src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-setup-result-envelope.v1.json`.

Subcommands:

  emit       Read context payload as JSON on stdin, derive missing
             fields (tools_added/removed, tier_changed), assemble the
             envelope, emit `SKF_SETUP_RESULT_JSON: {one-line JSON}`
             on stdout. Default subcommand.

  validate   Read an envelope (without the prefix) as JSON on stdin
             and verify it against the documented schema. No stdout
             on success; non-zero exit + stderr error on failure.
             Useful for paranoid pipelines that want to validate a
             received envelope before consuming it.

Context payload shape (consumed by `emit`):

  {
    "tier":                          "Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep",
    "previous_tier":                 "Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep|null",
    "tools":                         {"ast_grep": bool, "gh_cli": bool, "qmd": bool, "ccc": bool},
    "previous_tools":                {…same shape…|null},
    "config_path":                   "/abs/path/to/forge-tier.yaml",
    "ccc_index":                     {"status": "...", "indexed_path": "...|null", "file_count": int|null},
    "files_written":                 ["forge-tier.yaml", ...],
    "tier_override_active":          bool,
    "tier_override_invalid":         bool,
    "tier_override_invalid_value":   "string|null",
    "tier_override_invalid_suggestion": "string|null",
    "tier_override_unsafe":          bool,
    "tier_override_unsafe_missing":  ["gh", "qmd", ...],
    "require_tier_satisfied":        bool|null,
    "require_tier_failure_missing":  ["ccc", ...],
```

                                   SKILL.md

Validate SKILL.md frontmatter against agentskills.io specification.

Checks: frontmatter delimiters, name format (Unicode letters + digits +
hyphens, no consecutive/trailing hyphens), name-directory match,
description presence and length, compatibility length, and unknown fields.

Exit code 0 means pass (no high-severity issues).

positional arguments:

Writing happens here (before step-06 finalization) because `skill-check` is a file-based CLI — it reads artifacts from disk — so the files must exist before validation runs. Report any gaps or issues. Validation is advisory — issues are reported but do not block the workflow.

76	```
77	
78	This validates frontmatter, description, body limits, links, and formatting — and auto-fixes deterministic issues (field ordering, slug format, required fields, trailing newlines).

**Important finding from verification:** `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` is **NOT** a generic helper — it's hardcoded for `skf-setup` (its schema is `skf-setup-result-envelope.v1.json`, its input shape has `tier`/`tools`/`config_path`, etc.). The L6 scanner overstated reusability. Wiring it in for skf-quick-skill would require generalizing it (cross-skill blast radius) or building a new `skf-emit-quick-skill-result.py` — that's Phase 2 work, not Phase 1.

`skf-validate-frontmatter.py` IS reusable — that part of Rec #1 stands. I'll wire that in but skip the envelope-emitter wiring until Phase 2.

Branching off main and starting Phase 1.

32	Create or update the `active` pointer at `{skill_group}/active` pointing to `{version}` using the shared atomic-flip helper. The helper acquires an `flock` on `{skill_group}/active.skf-lock`, refuses to replace a non-link at `{skill_group}/active` (protecting against accidental `rm -rf` of a real directory), and uses a rename-over-symlink pattern so the update is atomic from a concurrent reader's perspective. On Windows the helper automatically falls back to a directory junction (`mklink /J`) when `os.symlink` fails with `PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD` / `ACCESS_DENIED` — junctions require no admin elevation and resolve identically for `skf-skill-inventory`'s consumers:
33	
34	**Resolve `{atomicWriteHelper}`:** probe `{atomicWriteProbeOrder}` (installed `_bmad/skf/...` first, dev `src/...` fallback); first existing path wins. HALT if neither candidate exists — the active-pointer flip MUST go through the atomic helper.

38	  --link {skill_group}/active \
39	  --target {version}

# Step 1: Resolve Target

## STEP GOAL:

To accept a GitHub URL or package name from the user, resolve it to a GitHub repository, detect the primary language, and prepare state for source extraction.

## Rules

- Focus only on resolving the target to a GitHub repository — do not begin extraction or compilation
- If resolution fails, hard halt with actionable guidance

## MANDATORY SEQUENCE

**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.

=== src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-02-ecosystem-check.md (head 20) ===
---
nextStepFile: './step-03-quick-extract.md'
---

# Step 2: Ecosystem Check

## STEP GOAL:

To query the agentskills.io ecosystem for an existing official skill matching the resolved target, preventing unnecessary duplication. This is an advisory gate — it never blocks the workflow on failure.

## Rules

- This check is advisory — never block the workflow on failure
- 5-second timeout on ecosystem queries; tool unavailability is a silent skip, not an error
- Do not begin extraction or compilation

## MANDATORY SEQUENCE

**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.

=== src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-03-quick-extract.md (head 20) ===
---
nextStepFile: './step-04-compile.md'
---

# Step 3: Quick Extract

## STEP GOAL:

To read the resolved GitHub repository source and extract the public API surface using surface-level source reading (no AST). Produces an extraction inventory of exports, descriptions, and manifest data for compilation.

## Rules

- Best-effort extraction — completeness is not required; surface-level reading only, no AST
- Do not begin compilation or write output files
- If no exports found, use README content as fallback

## MANDATORY SEQUENCE

**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.

=== src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md (head 20) ===
---
nextStepFile: './step-05-validate.md'
skillTemplateData: 'assets/skill-template.md'
---

# Step 4: Compile

## STEP GOAL:

To assemble the best-effort SKILL.md document, context-snippet.md in Vercel-aligned indexed format, and metadata.json with `source_authority: community` from the extraction inventory. Present compiled output for review before validation.

## Rules

- Focus only on assembling the three output documents — do not write files to disk (that's step-06)
- Follow template structure exactly from {skillTemplateData}
- Mark any sections with insufficient data as best-effort

## MANDATORY SEQUENCE

**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.

313	def check_prompt_basics(skill_path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], list[dict]]:
314	    """Check each prompt file for config header and progression conditions."""
315	    findings = []
316	    prompt_details = []
317	
318	    prompt_files = sorted(discover_step_files(skill_path), key=lambda p: _rel_step_path(skill_path, p))
319	    if not prompt_files:
320	        return prompt_details, findings
321	
322	    for f in prompt_files:
323	        content = f.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
324	        rel_path = _rel_step_path(skill_path, f)
325	        detail = {'file': rel_path, 'has_config_header': False, 'has_progression': False}
326	
327	        # Config header check
328	        if '{communication_language}' in content or '{document_output_language}' in content:
329	            detail['has_config_header'] = True
330	        else:
331	            findings.append({
332	                'file': rel_path, 'line': 1,
333	                'severity': 'medium', 'category': 'config-header',
334	                'issue': 'No config header with language variables found',
335	            })
336	
337	        # Progression condition check (look for progression-related keywords near end)
338	        lower = content.lower()
339	        prog_keywords = ['progress', 'advance', 'move to', 'next stage', 'when complete',
340	                         'proceed to', 'transition', 'completion criteria']
341	        if any(kw in lower for kw in prog_keywords):
342	            detail['has_progression'] = True
343	        else:
344	            findings.append({
345	                'file': rel_path, 'line': len(content.split('\n')),
346	                'severity': 'high', 'category': 'progression',
347	                'issue': 'No progression condition keywords found',
348	            })
349	
350	        # Directness checks
351	        for pattern, message in DIRECTNESS_PATTERNS:
352	            for m in re.finditer(pattern, content, re.IGNORECASE):
353	                line_num = content[:m.start()].count('\n') + 1
354	                findings.append({
355	                    'file': rel_path, 'line': line_num,
356	                    'severity': 'low', 'category': 'language',
357	                    'issue': message,
358	                })

### 3. Result Contract

Write the result contract per `shared/references/output-contract-schema.md`: the per-run record at `{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-{YYYYMMDD-HHmmss}.json` (UTC timestamp, resolution to seconds) and a copy at `{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-latest.json` (stable path for pipeline consumers — copy, not symlink). Include `SKILL.md`, `context-snippet.md`, and `metadata.json` paths in `outputs` and export count in `summary`.

### 4. Chain to Health Check

ONLY WHEN the active pointer has been created and the completion summary and result contract have been written will you then load, read the full file, and execute `{nextStepFile}`. The health-check step is the true terminal step — do not stop here even though the summary reads as final.
=== step-07 last lines ===

- Do not attempt any other action between loading this step and executing `{nextStepFile}`

## MANDATORY SEQUENCE

  "new_string": "**If skill-check is NOT available**, run the shared frontmatter validator instead of an LLM-walked checklist. Resolve `{frontmatterValidator}` by probing `{frontmatterValidatorProbeOrder}` (installed `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-validate-frontmatter.py` first, dev `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-frontmatter.py` fallback); first existing path wins. If neither candidate exists, log a high-severity issue (\"frontmatter validator unavailable — both `npx skill-check` and `skf-validate-frontmatter.py` missing\") and skip frontmatter validation.\n\n```bash\npython3 {frontmatterValidator} {skill_package}/SKILL.md --skill-dir-name {repo_name}\n```\n\nThe validator emits JSON with `status` (`pass`/`fail`), `issues[]` (each with `severity`, `code`, `message`), and `frontmatter` (the parsed name/description). It checks frontmatter delimiters, name format (Unicode letters + digits + hyphens, no consecutive/trailing hyphens), name-directory match, description presence and length, and unknown fields against the agentskills.io spec — the same shape this step would otherwise hand-walk. Record each `issues[]` entry as a validation issue with its reported severity. Missing frontmatter or missing required fields are high-severity — skills without valid frontmatter will fail `npx skills add` and `npx skill-check check`."

1	---
2	name: skf-quick-skill
3	description: Fast skill from a package name or GitHub URL — no brief needed. Use when the user requests a "quick skill" or "skill from URL" or "skill from package."
4	---
5	
6	# Quick Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	The fastest path to a skill — accept a GitHub URL or package name, resolve to source, extract the public API surface, and produce a best-effort SKILL.md with context snippet and metadata. No brief needed. Quick Skill is tier-unaware by design — all output is produced at community-tier quality regardless of available tools.
11	
12	## Role
13	
14	You are a rapid skill compiler collaborating with a developer. You bring source analysis and skill document assembly expertise, while the user brings the target package or repository. Work together efficiently — speed is the priority.
15	
16	## Workflow Rules
17	
18	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
19	
20	- Never fabricate content — all data must come from source extraction or user input
21	- Read each step file completely before taking any action
22	- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
23	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
24	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`
25	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
26	
27	## Stages
28	
29	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
30	|---|------|------|--------------|
31	| 1 | Resolve Target | steps-c/step-01-resolve-target.md | Yes |
32	| 2 | Ecosystem Check | steps-c/step-02-ecosystem-check.md | Yes |
33	| 3 | Quick Extract | steps-c/step-03-quick-extract.md | Yes |
34	| 4 | Compile | steps-c/step-04-compile.md | No (review) |
35	| 5 | Write & Validate | steps-c/step-05-validate.md | Yes |
36	| 6 | Finalize | steps-c/step-06-write.md | Yes |
37	| 7 | Workflow Health Check | steps-c/step-07-health-check.md | Yes |
38	
39	## Invocation Contract
40	
41	| Aspect | Detail |
42	|--------|--------|
43	| **Inputs** | target (GitHub URL or package name) [required], language_hint [optional], scope_hint [optional] |
44	| **Gates** | step-01: Input Gate [use args] | step-02: Choice Gate [P] (if match) | step-04: Review Gate [C] |
45	| **Outputs** | SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json, active symlink |
46	| **Headless** | All gates auto-resolve with default action when `{headless_mode}` is true |
47	
48	## On Activation
49	
50	1. Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and resolve:
51	   - `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `document_output_language`
52	   - `skills_output_folder`, `forge_data_folder`
53	
54	2. **Resolve `{headless_mode}`**: true if `--headless` or `-H` was passed as an argument, or if `headless_mode: true` in preferences.yaml. Default: false.
55	
56	3. Load, read the full file, and then execute `./steps-c/step-01-resolve-target.md` to begin the workflow.
57

45	
46	**Never `rm` + `ln -s` the active pointer manually.** The bare-rm pattern has two failure modes: (1) a concurrent reader sees a missing `active` mid-flip, and (2) a bug or typo that replaces `{skill_group}/active` with a plain directory turns the next manual `rm -rf {skill_group}/active` into data loss. The helper encapsulates both guards and the Windows junction fallback.

=== step-03 lines 25-95 ===
### 1. Read README

Read `README.md` from the repository root via web browsing.

Extract:
- **Description:** What the package does (first paragraph or tagline)
- **Features:** Key features or capabilities listed
- **Usage patterns:** Code examples showing common usage
- **Installation:** Package manager install command (confirms package name)

If README is unavailable, note and continue.

### 2. Read Manifest File

Based on detected language, read the primary manifest file:

- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** `package.json` — extract name, version, description, main, exports, dependencies
- **Python:** `pyproject.toml` or `setup.py` — extract project name, version, description, dependencies
- **Rust:** `Cargo.toml` — extract package name, version, description, dependencies
- **Go:** `go.mod` — extract module path, require list
- **Java (Maven):** `pom.xml` — extract `<groupId>`, `<artifactId>`, `<version>`, `<description>`, direct `<dependencies>`. For multi-module projects, also enumerate `<modules><module>` entries and read each submodule's `pom.xml` (treat each as a logical unit in the extraction inventory).
- **Kotlin / Java (Gradle):** `build.gradle.kts` or `build.gradle` — extract `group`, `version`, `description` (when declared), and top-level `dependencies { }` block. For multi-project builds, read `settings.gradle[.kts]` for `include(...)` entries and repeat per subproject.

Extract:
- **Package metadata:** name, version, description
- **Entry points:** main, exports, module fields
- **Key dependencies:** direct dependencies list

### 3. Scan Top-Level Exports

Based on language and entry points from manifest, read the primary export files:

**JavaScript/TypeScript:**
- Read `index.js`, `index.ts`, `src/index.ts`, or `main` field from package.json
- Extract: `export` statements, `module.exports` assignments
- Pattern: lines matching `export (const|function|class|default|type|interface)`

**Python:**
- Read `__init__.py` or `src/{package}/__init__.py`
- Extract: `__all__` list, top-level function/class definitions
- Pattern: lines matching `def |class |__all__`

**Rust:**
- Read `src/lib.rs`
- Extract: `pub fn`, `pub struct`, `pub enum`, `pub trait` declarations
- Pattern: lines matching `pub (fn|struct|enum|trait|mod)`

**Go:**
- Read exported functions from top-level `.go` files
- Extract: capitalized function names (Go export convention)
- Pattern: lines matching `func [A-Z]`

**Java:**
- Read `src/main/java/**/*.java` (focus on top-level packages declared in the manifest's `groupId`)
- Extract: public classes, public methods, and framework annotations that mark API surfaces (Spring, Jakarta EE, CDI)
- Pattern: lines matching `@(RestController|Service|Component|Configuration|Controller|Repository|Bean)|public (class|interface|enum|record) |public .* \(`
- **Multi-module Maven:** iterate the `<module>` entries discovered in §2 and repeat the scan per module, reading each `{module}/src/main/java/**/*.java`

**Kotlin:**
- Read `src/main/kotlin/**/*.kt` (Kotlin defaults to `public` visibility — omit `internal`/`private` declarations)
- Extract: top-level `fun`, `class`, `object`, `interface` declarations
- Pattern: lines matching `^(fun |class |object |interface |data class |sealed class |@(RestController|Service|Component|Configuration|Controller))`
- **Multi-project Gradle:** iterate the `include(...)` entries discovered in §2 and repeat the scan per subproject

**If scope_hint provided:** Focus reading on the specified directories instead of root.

### 4. Build Extraction Inventory

Assemble the extraction inventory from collected data:

=== step-01 §3 area ===

**Version targeting:** If the user input contains `@` followed by a semver-like string (e.g., `cognee@0.5.0`, `https://github.com/org/repo@2.1.0-beta`), parse it as:
- **Package/URL:** everything before the last `@`
- **Target version:** everything after the last `@`

Store the target version as `target_version` in the extraction context. When present, this version overrides auto-detection (same behavior as `target_version` in the skill-brief schema).

If no `@version` suffix is present, proceed as today — version will be auto-detected.

### 2. Classify Input Type

**If input starts with `https://github.com/` or `github.com/`:**
- Extract org/repo from URL
- Set `resolved_url` to the GitHub URL
- Set `repo_name` to the repo name (last path segment)
- Skip to step 4 (Detect Language)

**If input is a package name:**
- Proceed to step 3 (Registry Resolution)

### 3. Registry Resolution

Load {registryResolutionData} for resolution patterns.

**Execute the fallback chain in order — stop at first success:**

1. **npm registry:** Fetch `https://registry.npmjs.org/{package_name}` — extract `repository.url`
2. **PyPI registry:** Fetch `https://pypi.org/pypi/{package_name}/json` — extract `info.project_urls.Source` or `info.home_page`
3. **crates.io registry:** Fetch `https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/{package_name}` — extract `crate.repository`
4. **Web search fallback:** Search `"{package_name} github repository"` — look for GitHub URL

**If all methods fail — HARD HALT:**

"**Resolution failed.** Could not resolve `{package_name}` to a GitHub repository.

Check:

Classify Input Type\n\n**If input starts with `https://github.com/` or `github.com/`:**\n- Extract org/repo from URL\n- Set `resolved_url` to the GitHub URL\n- Set `repo_name` to the repo name (last path segment)\n- Skip to step 4 (Detect Language)\n\n**If input is a package-name-like token** (no whitespace, matches `[@a-zA-Z0-9._/-]+(@<semver>)?`, e.g. `lodash`, `@scope/name`, `requests==2.31`, `cognee@0.5.0`):\n- Proceed to step 3 (Registry Resolution)\n\n**Otherwise — input looks like free-form prose, not a target:**\n\nThe user typed something like \"I want a skill that helps with onboarding\" or \"build me a brainstorming workflow\" — quick-skill cannot resolve that to a GitHub repository. Instead of falling through to a registry-failure HARD HALT, redirect with a sibling-skill suggestion:\n\n\"**This input looks like a description, not a package or URL.** Quick Skill needs a package name (e.g. `lodash`, `@vercel/og`, `requests`) or a GitHub URL (e.g. `https://github.com/lodash/lodash`).\n\nIf you are describing a skill you want to **create from scratch** rather than compile from existing source:\n\n- Run `/skf-create-skill` with a skill brief — full pipeline with provenance tracking and AST-verified exports\n- Or use `bmad-agent-builder` for an interactive skill design session\n\nOtherwise, paste the package name or GitHub URL of the library you want to wrap, and quick-skill will resolve it.\"\n\n**GATE [default: HALT]** — In headless mode, emit the same redirect message and HALT with exit code 3 (resolution failure).
