```
## Assessment
**Skill type:** Multi-stage interactive workflow (7 stages, branching gates, headless mode, GitHub/registry integration). This is a complex workflow — context scaffolding matters because the executing agent must make routing, classification, and judgment calls (URL vs. package detection, language detection, ecosystem-match handling, write/overwrite, validation tier).
**Overview quality (SKILL.md):** Lean but functional. The Overview is a single 3-line paragraph (line 10) that establishes mission ("fastest path to a skill"), the input contract (URL or package name), the deliverables (SKILL.md + snippet + metadata), the no-brief speed positioning, and the one critical design rationale (`tier-unaware by design — community-tier quality regardless of available tools`). For a complex workflow this is on the thin edge — there is no theory-of-mind framing for the developer-user, and no domain framing for what "best-effort SKILL.md," "context-snippet," or "community tier" mean — but the Role section (line 14) and Workflow Rules (lines 18-25) carry meaningful framing, so the SKILL.md as a whole is contextual rather than under-contextualized. **Verdict: appropriate for the skill type.**
**Progressive disclosure:** Excellent. SKILL.md is 57 lines / ~705 tokens with a stages routing table, no inlined data tables, no fenced reference blocks, and clean delegation to `steps-c/*.md` per stage plus `references/registry-resolution.md` and `assets/skill-template.md`. Each stage prompt has a frontmatter `nextStepFile` chain that keeps SKILL.md out of the per-stage context.
**Synthesis:** This skill is well-crafted at the structural level — clean progressive disclosure, well-bounded stage prompts (each 1-7KB, single concern), explicit gates with headless-mode defaults documented inline, and consistent "MANDATORY SEQUENCE / CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE" envelope. The principal weaknesses are (1) prepass detected zero stage prompts because they live in `steps-c/` rather than at root — the prepass metric is misleading, the prompts are present and well-formed; (2) some defensive padding ("CRITICAL: Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise") repeats verbatim across all six step files; (3) the Invocation Contract gate cell uses `|` characters inside a markdown table cell that will render incorrectly. None of these threaten correctness — they are polish-level refinements.
## Prompt Health Summary
**Note on prepass discrepancy:** the prepass JSON reports `total_prompts: 0` and `prompts_with_config_header: 0` because its scanner only inspects root-level `*.md`. The seven step files in `steps-c/` are real prompts and were evaluated directly.
## Key Findings
### High
**H1 — Invocation Contract gate row breaks markdown table rendering**
**Why it matters:** The Invocation Contract is a load-bearing reference for headless callers — it documents the gates and their default actions. A garbled table is the row most likely to be scanned by integrators.
**Fix:** Replace inner `|` with `;` or `<br>`, or break the row into a sub-bullet list:
### Medium
**M1 — Repeated boilerplate "CRITICAL: Follow this sequence exactly" across all step files**
**Why it matters:** Low absolute waste but it dilutes the signal of the per-step `## Rules` block, which is where the genuinely step-specific constraints live (e.g., step-02's "5-second timeout; tool unavailability is a silent skip").
**Fix:** Either delete the repeated line entirely (`MANDATORY SEQUENCE` is self-explanatory) or move it to the workflow-rules section of SKILL.md once. Keep the per-step `## Rules` lists, which carry distinct content.
**M2 — Repeated boilerplate "ONLY WHEN ... will you load and read fully {nextStepFile}" tail**
**Why it matters:** Token waste and instruction noise. The agent should know how to read a file.
**Fix:** Keep the `CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE` (it's the canonical progression condition). Trim the `Menu Handling Logic` / `EXECUTION RULES` blocks to single-line directives: `On 'C': proceed to {nextStepFile}.` `On match P: proceed to {nextStepFile}.`
**M3 — step-04 `metadata.json` schema duplicated between step-04 and `assets/skill-template.md`**
**Why it matters:** Two sources of truth for the same schema invites drift; an authoring change to one will silently miss the other.
**Fix:** Pick one canonical location. The template already exists for this purpose — keep the schema in `assets/skill-template.md` and replace step-04's inline JSON with a list of just the population rules (e.g., `skf` version resolution chain, `confidence_tier: "Quick"`, `generated_by: "quick-skill"`).
**M4 — step-05 sections 4-7 are deterministic validators specified as prompt logic**
**Why it matters:** This is intelligence-placement — the L6 (script-opportunities) scanner will likely flag the same thing. LLM-driven structural validation is slower, non-reproducible, and prone to false-pass under context pressure.
**Fix:** When skill-check is available, treat its `diagnostics[]` as the source of truth and skip sections 5-7 (or reduce them to "verify skill-check covered these categories"). Keep manual fallback only for the `skill-check unavailable` branch.
**M5 — step-06 frontmatter contains a 6-line block comment explaining helper resolution**
**Why it matters:** Mild repetition; the body version is the one the agent actually reads in flow.
**Fix:** Drop the frontmatter comment, or alternately drop the body's "Resolve `{atomicWriteHelper}`" paragraph and link to the comment.
### Low
**L1 — Defensive imperatives in Workflow Rules**
**Fix:** Optional. If kept, consider rewriting positively: "Each step file is loaded just-in-time and gates progress to the next via `nextStepFile`."
**L2 — Step-01's "5. Confirm Resolution" prints a header and immediately auto-proceeds**
**Fix:** None required, or merge the resolution confirmation into step-03's extraction summary header so the user sees one continuous block of progress.
**L3 — `references/registry-resolution.md` partly duplicates step-01**
**Why it matters:** Same drift risk as M3.
**Fix:** Strip step-01 §3 to a one-line "Execute the fallback chain documented in {registryResolutionData}, stopping at first success" and let the reference own the per-registry details.
**L4 — Frontmatter `description` example uses second-person triggers**
### Note
**N1 — Prepass metric `total_prompts: 0` is a false negative**
## Strengths
- **Disciplined progressive disclosure.** SKILL.md stays at 57 lines because every detail lives in the right place: routing table for stages, frontmatter chain for stage-to-stage progression, dedicated reference for registry resolution, dedicated asset for output schemas. This is textbook structure for a multi-stage skill.
- **Self-contained stage prompts.** Each `steps-c/step-*.md` opens with `## STEP GOAL`, `## Rules`, and `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE`, and closes with `## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE`. None of them rely on SKILL.md being in context to execute correctly — strong context-compaction survival.
- **Explicit headless gates.** Every interactive gate is annotated with a `**GATE [default: X]**` line that specifies the auto-resolved choice, the log line, and (where relevant) the hard-halt fallback. The agent doesn't have to infer headless behavior — it's documented at the gate site, exactly where it's needed (e.g., step-01:35, step-02:70, step-04:171, step-05:38).
- **Real domain rationale.** Step-05 §1 explains *why* writing happens before validation (`skill-check` is file-based). Step-06 §1 explains *why* the active pointer must use the atomic helper (concurrency safety, Windows junction fallback, refusal-to-replace-non-link guardrail). Step-06 §4 explicitly notes the workflow does not stop at the completion summary because step-07 is the true terminal step. These rationale paragraphs are the kind of context that prevents an "optimizing" agent from undermining design constraints.
- **Conditional tool branching done right.** Step-05 §3 demonstrates the pattern correctly: empirical `npx skill-check -h` probe, then branch — never assumes availability, never fabricates a fallback that pretends to be the real validator.
- **Hard-halt messages are actionable.** Resolution failure (step-01:71-78), write failure (step-05:50-59), and active-pointer-not-symlink (step-06:42) all give the user concrete next-action guidance rather than just "error."
## File path reference
- `src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md`
- `src/skf-quick-skill/steps-c/step-01-resolve-target.md` … `step-07-health-check.md`
- `src/skf-quick-skill/references/registry-resolution.md`
```

Populate fields from extraction_inventory:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"name\": \"{skill_name}\",\n  \"version\": \"{extraction_inventory.version or 1.0.0}\",\n  \"description\": \"{brief description of the skill}\",\n  \"skill_type\": \"single\",\n  \"source_authority\": \"community\",\n  \"source_repo\": \"{resolved_url}\",\n  \"source_root\": \"{resolved_source_path}\",\n  \"source_commit\": \"{commit_sha_if_available}\",\n  \"source_package\": \"{package_name from manifest}\",\n  \"language\": \"{language}\",\n  \"generated_by\": \"quick-skill\",\n  \"generation_date\": \"{current ISO date}\",\n  \"confidence_tier\": \"Quick\",\n  \"spec_version\": \"1.3\",\n  \"exports\": [\"{export_1}\", \"{export_2}\"],\n  \"confidence_distribution\": {\n    \"t1\": 0,\n    \"t1_low\": \"{number of exports found — must be integer, not string}\",\n    \"t2\": 0,\n    \"t3\": 0\n  },\n  \"tool_versions\": {\n    \"ast_grep\": null,\n    \"qmd\": null,\n    \"skf\": \"{skf_version}\"   // Resolution chain: _bmad/skf/package.json → npm require → _bmad/skf/VERSION → \"unknown\"\n  },\n  \"stats\": {\n    \"exports_documented\": \"{number of exports found}\",\n    \"exports_public_api\": \"{number of exports found}\",\n    \"exports_internal\": 0,\n    \"exports_total\": \"{number of exports found}\",\n    \"public_api_coverage\": 1.0,\n    \"total_coverage\": 1.0,\n    \"scripts_count\": 0,\n    \"assets_count\": 0\n  },\n  \"dependencies\": [],\n  \"compatibility\": \"{semver-range or null}\",\n  \"provenance\": {\n    \"language_hint\": \"{language_hint or null}\",\n    \"scope_hint\": \"{scope_hint or null}\"\n  }\n}\n```",
  "new_string": "### 4. Generate Metadata JSON\n\nGenerate metadata.json following the **canonical schema in `{skillTemplateData}` § \"metadata.json Format\"** (loaded in §1). Use the template's exact field set, ordering, and types — do not duplicate the schema here. Apply these quick-skill-specific population rules on top of the template:\n\n- `confidence_tier`: `\"Quick\"` (constant)\n- `generated_by`: `\"quick-skill\"` (constant)\n- `source_authority`: `\"community\"` (constant)\n- `confidence_distribution.t1_low`: number of exports found — **integer, not string**. Other distribution buckets stay at `0`.\n- `tool_versions.skf`: resolved from `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/package.json` → npm require → `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/VERSION` → `\"unknown\"` (first hit wins)\n- `tool_versions.ast_grep` / `tool_versions.qmd`: `null` (Quick is tier-unaware)\n- `stats.exports_documented` / `exports_public_api` / `exports_total`: equal to the count of exports detected in step-03 (integers); `exports_internal: 0`; `public_api_coverage: 1.0`; `total_coverage: 1.0`; `scripts_count` / `assets_count`: `0` for Quick\n- `provenance.language_hint` / `provenance.scope_hint`: echo the user-supplied hints from step-01 (or `null` when omitted)\n- `version`: `extraction_inventory.version` or `\"1.0.0\"`\n- `generation_date`: current ISO 8601 UTC datetime\n- `exports[]`: list of detected export names from `extraction_inventory.exports`\n\nIf a field is added to the template's metadata.json schema in the future, it lands here automatically — these rules describe **how Quick populates** the template, not what fields exist."
}

{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"chore(skf-quick-skill): quality scan Phase 1 — broken paths, structure, latency, UX redirect\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\nPhase 1 of the `skf-quick-skill` quality remediation triggered by the 2026-05-01 quality scan (grade: **Good**). Addresses every \"broken\" item plus the medium-severity themes that don't require new shared scripts (Phase 2) or new headless orchestration primitives (Phase 3).\n\n7 focused commits:\n\n1. **`fix`** — Prefix the 2 bare `_bmad`/`src` refs with `{project-root}/` (was: 2 high-severity broken). Path-standards lint clean.\n2. **`chore`** — Add `{communication_language}` config header to all 7 step files; add the standard `proceed to` progression vocabulary to step-02/06/07's CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE so the workflow-integrity scanner can detect intent; replace polite \"Please\" / vague \"When ready\" with direct imperatives. Resolves all **13 prepass findings** (3 high, 7 medium, 3 low).\n3. **`refactor`** — Wire `skf-validate-frontmatter.py` into step-05's manual fallback (was a five-bullet hand-walked checklist); installed `_bmad/skf/...` first, dev `src/...` fallback. ~150-250 tokens saved per fallback run.\n4. **`docs`** — SKILL.md `Outputs` row now includes the result contract; Invocation Contract `Gates` row's inner `|` separators escaped; `scope_hint` / `language_hint` now echo through `metadata.json` (`provenance` block) and step-06's completion summary.\n5. **`perf`** — Parallel-fetch directive in step-03 (README + manifest + entry-points + multi-module submodules in one batched message); collapse step-05's `npx skill-check` into one invocation (security scan rides on §4); 10s/15s per-call timeouts on the step-01 registry chain; SKILL.md activation reads `config.yaml` + `preferences.yaml` in parallel.\n6. **`feat`** — Confused-user redirect in step-01 §2: free-form prose input (\"I want a skill that helps with onboarding\") now gets a sibling-skill suggestion (`/skf-create-skill`, `bmad-agent-builder`) instead of burning the registry chain to land on a generic \"Resolution failed\". Highest-impact single UX fix per the report.\n7. **`refactor`** — Trim repeated `**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence...` boilerplate (already in SKILL.md Workflow Rules); drop multi-line frontmatter prose comments superseded by body text; consolidate `metadata.json` schema in `assets/skill-template.md` as the single source of truth (step-04 § 4 now lists population rules only).\n\n## Quality scan deltas\n\n| Theme | Before | After (expected) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Broken | 2 high | 0 |\n| Stage prompt structural conventions (Theme 2) | 13 findings | 0 (prepass clean) |\n| Wall-clock latency (Theme 4) | 4 findings | 0 (directives + parallel-reads landed) |\n| SKILL.md Invocation Contract / Outputs | 2 findings | 0 |\n| Edge-case (confused-user redirect) | 1 highest-impact | resolved |\n| Intelligence placement (Theme 1) | 10 findings | 1 resolved (frontmatter validator); 9 deferred to Phase 2 (new shared scripts) |\n| Headless / CI primitives (Theme 3) | 5 findings | deferred to Phase 3 |\n| Edge-case coverage (Theme 5 remainder) | 6 findings | deferred to Phase 4 (the report itself flags these as \"phase opportunistically\") |\n\n## Notes & honesty checks\n\n- **`skf-emit-result-envelope.py` was NOT wired in** as the report's Recommendation #1 suggested. On verification, the helper is hardcoded for `skf-setup` (its schema is `skf-setup-result-envelope.v1.json`, input shape is setup-specific). Wiring it would require generalizing it — that's Phase 2 work. The L6 scanner overstated its reusability.\n- Step-05 §5/§6/§7 still hand-walk validators when `skill-check` IS available. The prompt-craft scanner (M4) and L6 (F7) flagged this; the right fix is to treat skill-check's `diagnostics[]` as authoritative, which dovetails with Phase 2's script extraction. Left in place for now.\n- The Theme 2 fix added the `proceed to` keyword to existing CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE blocks rather than restructuring them as labelled `Progression` subsections. Substance is preserved; the prepass detector is satisfied; the more invasive restructuring can wait if the next scan still flags it.\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] `npm run quality` passes locally end-to-end (formatters, linters, schemas, install/cli/workflow/python/knowledge tests, validate:schemas/skills/refs, docs:validate-drift)\n- [x] `prepass-workflow-integrity.py` returns 0 issues against `src/skf-quick-skill/`\n- [x] `scan-path-standards.py` returns 0 issues against `src/skf-quick-skill/`\n- [ ] CI green on PR\n- [ ] Re-run full quality scan after merge to confirm grade lifts\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Open Phase 1 PR"
}

> AGENT

Phase 1 PR: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/259

**Status check before Phase 2:**

Phase 1 ships the broken-item fixes, all 13 prepass findings, all wall-clock latency wins, the highest-impact UX redirect, the SKILL.md contract docs, and the boilerplate cleanup. It's a meaningful grade-lift on its own.

**Phase 2 scope** (new shared Python scripts) is genuinely substantial and would be its own multi-PR effort:
- `skf-resolve-registry.py` — registry chain + version parsing + language detection by manifest (folds F1/F9/F10)
- `skf-extract-manifest.py` — multi-format manifest parsing (F2)
- `skf-scan-exports.py` — multi-language export regex (F3)
- `skf-compile-metadata.py` — strict-shape metadata.json compilation (F4)

Each needs unit tests under the script tests/ tree, careful path-resolution that matches the existing `skf-atomic-write.py` / `skf-validate-frontmatter.py` conventions, and prompt rewires in steps 01/03/04. Realistically that's 6-10 hours of work and would benefit from being its own focused session — especially since these scripts could be reused by `skf-create-skill`, `skf-create-stack-skill`, etc., so the design needs to be generalized rather than quick-skill-specific.

**My honest recommendation:** merge Phase 1 first, look at the new quality-scan grade, and decide whether Phase 2 is worth doing now or scheduling. The quality scanner may already say "Excellent" once Phase 1 lands — the heavy weight in the rubric was on the prepass findings and broken items, both fully resolved.

Want me to:
- (a) wait for Phase 1 review/merge and then re-run the quality scan to see where we actually stand,
- (b) keep going on Phase 2 right now (medium-effort, might overshoot what's actually needed),
- (c) start Phase 3 (headless CI primitives — also medium effort, more architectural)?

## Quality Scan v2 — Post-Phase-1

**Grade: Good** (unchanged) | **Broken: 0** (was 2) | **Total findings: 23** (was ~39)

> "skf-quick-skill is a tightly scoped, well-disciplined seven-stage workflow with strong purpose alignment, exemplary headless discipline, and **above-average intelligence-placement: the heaviest deterministic operations (frontmatter validation, security scan, atomic symlink flip) are already script-driven**."

### What Phase 1 actually moved

| Theme | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Broken | 2 high | **0** ✅ |
| Stage prompt structural conventions | 13 | **0** ✅ (theme dropped) |
| Wall-clock latency | 4 | 3 (some remain) |
| Edge-case (confused-user redirect, etc.) | 7 | 5 |
| Intelligence placement | 10 | 5 (frontmatter validator landed; 5 still need scripts) |

Three new low-priority themes surfaced now that the higher-noise ones cleared (SKILL.md surface drift, prompt/reference content dedup, headless result-contract stdout). That's expected scanner behavior — finer-grained issues become visible after coarse ones go.

### Why the grade didn't lift to Excellent

One **high-severity theme remains:** "Intelligence Misplacement — LLM does deterministic regex/JSON-walking work that belongs in scripts" with 5 findings. The grade rubric appears to cap at "Good" while any high-severity theme exists. **The only path to Excellent is Phase 2's actual script-extraction work** (~6-10h, real engineering).

### Honesty note

The L6 scanner once again recommended wiring `skf-emit-result-envelope.py` into step-06 §3 — same hallucination as the v1 scan. The script is hardcoded for `skf-setup`, not reusable as-is. Worth knowing: scanners can be confidently wrong about specific helper reusability, so verify before acting on script-recommendation findings.

## My recommendation: **stop here.**

Phase 1 is a real, measurable improvement: 0 broken items, 40% fewer total findings, all 13 prepass findings cleared, clear narrative-level praise from the new scan, and concrete UX/perf wins shipped (confused-user redirect, parallel fetches, registry timeouts, scope_hint provenance).

The remaining "high" theme is genuine engineering work (3 new shared Python scripts + cross-skill design), not cleanup. The "Excellent" grade requires it but isn't a goal in itself — it's a side effect of doing structurally-good work that benefits multiple skills. Doing it autonomously, right now, in one push, would mean rushing it; that's how scripts ship with bad APIs that other skills then depend on.

### Three concrete options

**A. Accept the win** (recommended). Move on. Quality scan delta is your audit trail.

**B. Cherry-pick the 2-3 cheap remaining wins** (~30 min): SKILL.md surface drift fixes (Rec #8), step-01 monorepo detection hint (part of Rec #3, low-cost subset), `RESULT_CONTRACT=<path>` stdout line (Rec #7 first item). None move the grade, all are clean polish.

**C. Schedule Phase 2 properly** as a separate, focused multi-PR effort on a quiet day — design the 3 new shared scripts with `skf-create-skill` / `skf-create-stack-skill` as co-consumers, write tests, do it right.

Want me to `/schedule` an agent to re-run the quality scan in ~3 months to catch drift, or are we done with this skill?
